## OPEN rows — what resolves them (all stay OPEN, none flipped to UNREACHABLE)

- Rows 1, 3, 4 (writeToParcel / createFromParcel / newArray): one printed marshalling site for AddTorrentParams (an Intent/Bundle putExtra/getParcelableExtra naming AddTorrentParams, or a fragment-arguments site), or a saved full-repo grep for `AddTorrentParams` parcel use. Row 3's resolution is shared with batch 21 row 36 — see Caveats.
- Row 2 (`$1.<init>`) is deliberately NOT OPEN on the same grounds: its construction site (the CREATOR field at :109-119) is printed, and class initialisation follows from the printed AddTorrentParams construction sites. ## Caveats the merger must see (recorded, not decided)

1. **Strict-entry:** no launcher step was printed in this pass. Engine rows anchor at TorrentService (init/stopEngine, themselves called from onStartCommand/onCreate) and at MainViewModel/DetailTorrentViewModel/AddTorrentViewModel/FeedDownloaderWorker. Under a strict launcher-entry rule all 37 REACHABLE rows revert to OPEN on that point alone.
2. **Parcelable convention (rows 1–4 + batch 21 rows 36–38):** this file keeps `$1.<init>` REACHABLE on its printed CREATOR construction site while rows 1, 3, 4 stay OPEN for lack of a printed marshalling site; batch 21 keeps `<init>(Parcel)` REACHABLE on the printed CREATOR.createFromParcel body (:112). The merger must pick one convention and apply it consistently across batches 21–22: either (a) CREATOR presence + class construction suffices for all parcel rows (then 22/1, 22/3, 22/4, 21/38 flip to REACHABLE), or (b) a printed marshalling site is required for all parcel rows (then 22/2 and 21/36 flip to OPEN). Labels in this file follow the split convention and are flagged, not decided.
3. **Lambda $n mapping (rows 6–8):** source-order only, NOT bytecode-verified. The plan descriptors for $1 (`(TorrentEngineListener)V`) and $2 (`(CompletableEmitter)V`) do not naively match the Runnable/Action bodies at :50-53 and :82-83, so the merger must confirm the $n-to-body mapping against bytecode before merging. Labels are unaffected: all three bodies sit inside the single printed listen() body, and each runs within the listen() flow (create body on subscribe, eventHandler on finish/remove events, dispose action on dispose via doStop:296).
4. **confirmed_by:** per-row convention stated at the top. Rows 11–13 read INFERRED here because no printed app code names them (session dispatch); under a "registration printed ⇒ SOURCE" convention they would read SOURCE. Labels unaffected.
5. **Overload traps avoided:** `TorrentEngine.addTorrent(AddTorrentParams,boolean)` :329 is NOT row 20/21 (it is the non-plan overload feeding row 25); `stopRecording`/`write` overloads in Logger are not batch 22 rows; `TorrentEngine.start()` (FeedDownloaderWorker :198/:204) is not a batch 22 row.

- confirmed_by convention: SOURCE = a direct app-code call site naming the method is printed; INFERRED = the method runs only through unprinted framework/library dispatch (session listener dispatch, Rx/Flowable/Single subscription) where definition and registration are printed. Per-row convention, not whole-route; labels unaffected.
- Strict-entry caveat: as batches 23-24 — routes anchor at ViewModels / TorrentService, not at a printed launcher step. Under a strict launcher-entry rule all REACHABLE rows revert to OPEN; the merger must pick one convention, NOT decided here.
- Anonymous-class numbering: $1 = observeEngineRunning listener, $2 = createFetchMagnetSingle listener, $3 = observeTorrentMetaInfo listener, $4 = TorrentFileObserver listener, $5 = engineListener — as in the input proposal and fresh TorrentEngine.java; $ numbers are source-order matches, NOT bytecode-verified, labels unaffected by a swap. $1/$2/$3 rows are upgraded here only because their enclosing methods are now REACHABLE on freshly printed external callers (see rows 10-16); if the merger rejects an enclosing method's caller, its $ rows revert to OPEN with it.
- Why no UNREACHABLE / no OPEN: every row now has a printed caller or a printed enclosing REACHABLE method.

## confirmed_by convention used in this file (labels unaffected by the convention choice)

- SOURCE = a printed app-code line names the method as a call target: a direct call, a
  `notifyListeners` call site naming the callback, or a printed `new X(...)` / anonymous
  instantiation site for a `<init>` row.
- INFERRED = the method is reached only through unprinted framework/library/Rx/JVM dispatch
  (libtorrent alert delivery to a notify site, Rx `Flowable.create`/thread dispatch, JVM class
  initialisation), even though its body and its registration are printed.
- Never `DYN`, `FW-MEMORY`, or `UNVERIFIED` as confirmed_by. OPEN rows would show — (none);
  there are no OPEN rows in this batch.
- Lambda `$n` numbering is matched to plan JVM descriptors, NOT bytecode-verified. A swap of two
  same-descriptor lambdas would not change any label in this batch, because every lambda in
  rows 32–40 belongs to a flowable whose subscription route is printed.

2. **Engine listener registration.** TorrentEngine (fresh) :140
   `session.addListener(engineListener)`; engineListener field :1265
   `new TorrentEngineListener() { ... }` with the five batch-26 overrides at :1291
   (onTorrentLoaded), :1306 (onTorrentFinished), :1329 (onTorrentMoving), :1348 (onTorrentMoved),
   :1420 (onTorrentMetadataLoaded). Trap avoided: TorrentEngine :592 `onTorrentMetadataLoaded`
   is a *different* anonymous listener inside `observeTorrentMetaInfo`, not the $5 override, and
   is not cited for any batch-26 row.
3. **Session listener registration and the base-method mechanism.** TorrentSessionImpl (fresh):
   `torrentTaskListener` :1053–1059 overrides **only** `onTorrentRemoved`; it is added in
   `onBeforeStart` :986 and removed in `onBeforeStop` :1036. Therefore, for every event except
   `onTorrentRemoved`, a notify during a running session reaches the **base** (empty)
   `TorrentEngineListener` method through `torrentTaskListener` (Java virtual dispatch to the
   unoverridden base method is the one INFERRED step; registration and every notify site are
   printed). For `onTorrentRemoved`, the base method runs through `engineListener`, which does
   **not** override it (TorrentEngine :1265–1454 override list). For `onSessionStopped`,
   `torrentTaskListener` has already been removed in `onBeforeStop`, so the base method runs
   through `engineListener`, which also does not override it.
4. **Notify loops (fresh).** TorrentSessionImpl `notifyListeners` :1445–1451 iterates `listeners`;
   TorrentDownloadImpl `notifyListeners` :187–193 does the same over the shared queue
   (TorrentSessionImpl `newTask` passes the session `listeners` queue to new downloads).
5. **The batch-26 OPEN-row closure (the change from the prior proposal).**
   DetailTorrentFragment (fresh) `subscribeTorrentInfo` :251, called from `onStart` :228,
   subscribes `viewModel.observeTorrentInfoPair()` :265 and `viewModel.observeAdvancedTorrentInfo()`
   :271. DetailTorrentViewModel (fresh) `observeTorrentInfo` :167–168 calls
   `infoProvider.observeInfo(torrentId)` (observeTorrentInfoPair :191–192 combines it), and
   `observeAdvancedTorrentInfo` :171–172 calls `infoProvider.observeAdvancedInfo(torrentId)`.
   TorrentInfoProvider (fresh) `observeInfo` :94–95 calls `makeInfoFlowable`, and
   `observeAdvancedInfo` :106–107 calls `makeAdvancedInfoFlowable`. Hence every lambda inside
   those two flowables (rows 32–40) is subscribed through a printed chain, and none stays OPEN.
   The prior proposal's missing evidence is no longer missing.

## Strict-entry caveat (explicit, all REACHABLE rows)

Routes are anchored at TorrentService (a Service entry, not a launcher entry) and at
DetailTorrentFragment / MainFragment / MainActivity onStart/subscribe steps. Which component
starts TorrentService, and the launcher → DetailTorrentFragment / MainActivity step, were not
printed in this pass. Under a strict launcher-entry rule all 40 REACHABLE rows revert to OPEN.
Under the anchor convention used by sibling detail/main batches, they are REACHABLE as tabled.
The merger must pick one strict-entry convention for all batches; this file does not decide it.

## confirmed_by convention used in this file (labels unaffected by the convention choice)

- SOURCE = a printed app-code line names the method as a call target (an `observe*` method called
  by a ViewModel/Fragment/Activity method, or a `make*` method called by its `observe*` method).
- INFERRED = the method is a lambda whose body is printed but whose invocation crosses unprinted
  RxJava `Flowable.create` / `Observable.interval` / thread dispatch, even though its enclosing
  flowable's subscription route is printed.
- Never `DYN`, `FW-MEMORY`, or `UNVERIFIED` as confirmed_by.
- Lambda `$n` numbering is matched to plan JVM descriptors (captured variables + arguments, see
  the descriptor notes in the table), NOT bytecode-verified. The descriptor check is what pins
  each `$n` to its body: e.g. `$0` in batch 26 is the handleEvent consumer because its descriptor
  (String, AtomicReference, FlowableEmitter, String) is exactly its captured variables plus its
  argument. A swap of two same-descriptor lambdas (batch 27 rows 6/7, both (SingleEmitter)) is
  resolved by body, not descriptor alone: row 6 is the `Single.create` body, row 7 the thread body
  inside it — both run on the same subscription route, so a swap would not change either label.

## confirmed_by convention used in this file (labels unaffected by the convention choice)

- SOURCE = a printed app-code line names the method as a call target: a direct call, a
  `notifyListeners` call site naming a listener callback, a printed `new X(...)` / anonymous
  instantiation site for a `<init>` row, or a printed call inside a flowable body (e.g.
  `TorrentInfoProvider:331 !newInfo.equals(oldInfo)` for AdvancedTorrentInfo.equals).
- INFERRED = the method is reached only through unprinted framework/library/JVM dispatch:
  JVM class initialisation (`<clinit>`), or a `<init>` whose instantiation happens only inside
  a `<clinit>` (AdvancedTorrentInfo$1, instantiated by the static CREATOR field initializer).
- Anonymous listener `<init>` rows ($1–$4) are SOURCE, because their anonymous instantiation
  sites are printed outside any `<clinit>` (TorrentInfoProvider :147, :253, :457, :478).
  The Creator `<init>` row (31) is INFERRED, because its only instantiation is the CREATOR
  field initializer inside row 22's `<clinit>`.
- Never `DYN`, `FW-MEMORY`, or `UNVERIFIED` as confirmed_by. OPEN rows show — (none).
- Anonymous-class mapping is by file order in the fresh TorrentInfoProvider print, NOT
  bytecode-verified: $1 = listener in `makeInfoFlowable` (:147), $2 = listener in
  `makeInfoListFlowable` (:253), $3 = listener in `makeTorrentsDeletedFlowable` (:457),
  $4 = listener in `makeSessionStatsFlowable` (:478). The override sets match the plan rows
  exactly ($1: stateChanged, paused, restoreSessionError, torrentError, sessionStats — no
  onTorrentRemoved; $2: all six including onTorrentRemoved; $3: only onTorrentRemoved;
  $4: only onSessionStats), which corroborates the mapping independently of file order.

## Strict-entry caveat (explicit, all REACHABLE rows)

Routes are anchored at ViewModel / provider methods and MainActivity, not at a fully printed
launcher chain; the Activity → ViewModel step and (for $1) the DetailTorrentFragment → ViewModel
step beyond `subscribeTorrentInfo` were not all freshly printed here (DetailTorrentFragment
itself is fresh: `subscribeTorrentInfo` :251 subscribes `observeTorrentInfoPair` :265 and
`observeAdvancedTorrentInfo` :271, which covers the $1 anchor). Under a strict launcher-entry
rule all 27 REACHABLE rows revert to OPEN. Under the anchor convention used by sibling
detail/main batches, they are REACHABLE as tabled. The merger must pick one strict-entry
convention for all batches; this file does not decide it.

## Parcelable policy used in this batch (consistent with batch 22 precedent)

A Parcelable framework method (`createFromParcel`, `newArray`, `describeContents`,
`writeToParcel`, a Parcel ctor) is REACHABLE only with a printed parcel/unparcel site, not
from the `Parcelable` interface alone and not from a CREATOR field alone. A method whose only
printed caller is itself an OPEN Parcelable/framework method inherits OPEN (rows 19, 21, 23).
A `Comparable`/`hashCode`/`toString` method is REACHABLE only with a printed sort, hash-collection,
or log/concatenation caller, not from the interface alone (rows 25, 28, 29). No OPEN row here is
flipped to UNREACHABLE: no saved full-repo grep was run, and no `search_code` 0 result was used
as evidence of absence.

## Correction to the prior proposal (label unchanged)

Row 18, `AbstractInfoParcel.<init>()`: the prior proposal stated that the printed subclasses
call only `super(String)` / `super(Parcel)`. That is true for TorrentInfo, PeerInfo, and
TrackerInfo in the local copies checked, but it misses one printed super-call: the freshly
printed `AdvancedTorrentInfo()` no-arg ctor :49–52 explicitly calls `super()`. The label does
NOT change, because `AdvancedTorrentInfo()` itself has no printed caller in any file printed in
this session (the only printed `new AdvancedTorrentInfo(...)` site, TorrentEngine :846–860, is
the 12-arg ctor, row 24; no `new AdvancedTorrentInfo()` no-arg site was found in the printed
files). Row 18 therefore stays OPEN, now with the correct reason: its sole printed super-call
site is itself an uncalled ctor, not because no super-call exists. A printed no-arg
construction site flips both row 18 and the (non-batch-28) `AdvancedTorrentInfo()` row together.

## Conventions (recorded, not decided — merger must pick project-wide)

1. **Strict-entry:** no launcher step was freshly printed in this pass. Every REACHABLE row above is anchored at session/engine/provider/download sites and **reverts to OPEN under a strict launcher-entry rule** (printed launcher → host step required). Do not claim a launcher anchor from a sibling batch's print.
2. **Per-row confirmed_by** (see table header). Under a whole-route INFERRED convention, SOURCE rows reached through unprinted alert/Rx dispatch (notably rows 11, 27, 30) would read INFERRED; labels unchanged.
3. **Parcelable Creator `$1.<init>`:** INFERRED when the enclosing class is first used on a printed route, because the CREATOR field initializer runs at class init (JVM timing unprinted). This is applied uniformly to rows 5, 20, 38. It does **not** extend to `createFromParcel`/`newArray`, which run only on framework unparcel.
4. **`<clinit>`:** INFERRED when any method/ctor/field of the class is used on a printed route (class init precedes first use). Applied to rows 8, 23, 29.
5. **Parcelable-trio policy:** `writeToParcel` / `describeContents` / Parcel ctor / `$1.createFromParcel` / `$1.newArray` stay OPEN unless a printed app-side parcel/unparcel site is found (Intent/Bundle `putExtra`/`getParcelableExtra`, `writeParcelable`, or a printed unparcel site). A CREATOR definition alone is not a caller.
6. **Library-dispatch equals:** `contains`/`containsAll`/`remove` on a List dispatch to element `equals` by library code, so such rows are INFERRED, not SOURCE (row 15). An explicit printed `x.equals(y)` call is SOURCE (batch 30 row 7, batch 29 row 34).
7. **No OPEN → UNREACHABLE flip on absence** without a saved full-repo grep in rule-F/C5 form. The greps reported above are over the fetched subset only and prove nothing elsewhere.
8. **Dependency rule:** a method whose only printed caller sits inside an OPEN method does not become REACHABLE (rows 9, 21, 31, 39 illustrate the Parcel-ctor/CREATOR loop; row 25 illustrates the converter's deliberate non-use).

## confirmed_by convention used here

All rows are marked INFERRED, deliberately, following the batch-2
merge-ready precedent. Reason: an UNREACHABLE proposal on printed files
alone rests on (a) a printed definition, (b) printed caller sites that use
a *different* overload or a *different* method, and (c) no caller printed
for this exact overload in the files fetched this session — but no saved
full-repo grep exists, so no UNREACHABLE row is claimed SOURCE. An OPEN row
was INFERRED for the same reason (evidence incomplete, not a printed direct
route); 2026-11-01 D1 revision: row 26 OPEN is now —, cosmetic only. No row uses DYN, FW-MEMORY, or UNVERIFIED as confirmed_by. If the
merger adopts a per-row SOURCE convention for rows whose definition +
overload contrast are both freshly printed, labels are unaffected; only
confirmed_by would change, and the merger must still run the saved
full-repo grep before flipping any OPEN row to UNREACHABLE.

## Headline correction to the audit — row 31 is UNREACHABLE, not REACHABLE (flipped from OPEN in the 2026-11-01 revision)

The audit's hand route for row 31 (`s3_route_priority_final.txt`, step 12)
cites `PriorityConverter.java:94-101` and `TorrentSessionImpl.java:1541-1575`
(`:1574 convert(priority).swig()`). That is the **app→lib** single overload,
`convert(Priority)` at PriorityConverter.java:94
(`public static org.libtorrent4j.Priority convert(@NonNull Priority priority)`),
where `Priority` is the app class. Row 31's plan descriptor is the opposite
overload, **lib→app** single convert at PriorityConverter.java:103
(`public static Priority convert(@NonNull org.libtorrent4j.Priority priority)`),
descriptor `(Lorg/libtorrent4j/Priority;)Lorg/proninyaroslav/libretorrent/core/model/data/Priority;`.
All six convert call sites printed fresh in TorrentDownloadImpl.java and
TorrentSessionImpl.java are the other three overloads, not row 31:
- array app→lib (:54) — TorrentDownloadImpl.java:731
- single app→lib (:94) — TorrentDownloadImpl.java:1139, TorrentSessionImpl.java:1574
- array lib→app (:74) — TorrentDownloadImpl.java:1646, TorrentSessionImpl.java:437, :451
No printed caller for single lib→app (:103) appears in any file fetched
this session. The route trunk (AddTorrentActivity → AddTorrentViewModel
Thread → TorrentEngine.addTorrentSync → TorrentSessionImpl.addTorrent →
download, with STATE `isRunning()` at TorrentEngine.java:409-412) is
consistent with reaching `download()`, but its final step misidentifies
the overload, so it does **not** establish row 31. Row 31 therefore stays
OPEN (named grep target below), and must NOT be flipped to UNREACHABLE
without a saved full-repo grep, per the task rule. If the merger's grep
finds a single-arg lib→app caller, flip row 31 to REACHABLE; if a saved
full-repo grep finds none, only then flip it to UNREACHABLE
`no_trigger_in_app`.

## Conventions (recorded, not decided — merger must pick project-wide)

1. **Strict-entry:** no launcher step was freshly printed in this pass. Every REACHABLE row above is anchored at engine/provider/download/ViewModel sites and **reverts to OPEN under a strict launcher-entry rule**. In particular, rows 21/22/26/28/34 are detail-anchored (detail tap not freshly printed) and row 38 is dialog-anchored (AddFeedDialog show step not freshly printed); they stand only under the anchor convention used here.
2. **Per-row confirmed_by** (see table header). Under a whole-route INFERRED convention, SOURCE rows reached through unprinted dispatch would read INFERRED; labels unchanged.
3. **Parcelable Creator `$1.<init>`:** INFERRED when the enclosing class is first used on a printed route, because the CREATOR field initializer runs at class init (JVM timing unprinted). Applied to rows 11, 31. It does **not** extend to `createFromParcel`/`newArray`, which run only on framework unparcel.
4. **`<clinit>`:** INFERRED when any method/ctor/field of the class is used on a printed route. Applied to rows 1, 14, 18, 36.
5. **Parcelable-trio policy:** `writeToParcel` / `describeContents` / Parcel ctor / `$1.createFromParcel` / `$1.newArray` stay OPEN unless a printed app-side parcel/unparcel site is found. A CREATOR definition alone is not a caller.
6. **Library-dispatch equals:** `containsAll` dispatches to element `equals` by library code, so row 25 is INFERRED, not SOURCE. An explicit printed `x.equals(y)` call is SOURCE (row 7).
7. **Room-generated instantiation policy:** a Room `@Entity`'s Parcel ctor (row 37) or any ctor is not flipped to REACHABLE on entity status alone; only a printed construction or a printed generated-instantiation step counts. (Row 38 is REACHABLE on a printed `new FeedChannel`, not on entity status.)
8. **Dependency rule:** a method whose only printed caller sits inside an OPEN method does not become REACHABLE. Rows 16/17 stand/fall with rows 2/10; row 2's internal `TagInfo.CREATOR` use at TorrentInfo.java:140 does not bootstrap TagInfo (batch 31 row 19/27 rest on the equals routes, not on this site).
9. **No OPEN → UNREACHABLE flip on absence** without a saved full-repo grep in rule-F/C5 form.

## Conventions (recorded, not decided — merger must pick project-wide)

1. **Strict-entry:** no launcher step was freshly printed in this pass. Every REACHABLE row above is anchored at session/parser/ViewModel/filter sites and **reverts to OPEN under a strict launcher-entry rule**. In particular, the TagInfo equals routes rest on the filter lambda (TorrentFilterCollection:98) and `removeTag` (AddTorrentViewModel:163), whose own callers were not freshly printed in this pass.
2. **Per-row confirmed_by** (see table header). Under a whole-route INFERRED convention, SOURCE rows would read INFERRED; labels unchanged.
3. **Parcelable Creator `$1.<init>` / `<clinit>` treatment (the batch 29–31 convention, applied uniformly here and in batches 29–30):** `$1.<init>` is INFERRED when the enclosing class is first used on a printed route, because the CREATOR field initializer runs at class init; `<clinit>` is INFERRED when any method/ctor/field of the class is used on a printed route. This **corrects** the prior batch 31 OPEN verdicts for rows 4, 16, 19, 27 **only because** the enclosing classes' first uses are freshly printed in this pass (rows 4/16: construction sites; rows 19/27: equals triggers). If the merger rejects this treatment, rows 4, 16, 19, 27 revert to OPEN together with the convention — and row 19/27 additionally revert if row 24's library-dispatch equals route is rejected. Prior proposals were inconsistent on this point (batch 29 row 5 MagnetInfo$1.\<init\> was SOURCE, batch 30 TorrentInfo$1.\<init\> INFERRED, batch 31 rows 4/16/27 OPEN); the merger must pick **one** treatment for all Creator `$1.<init>` rows project-wide, and this file's treatment is the candidate stated here, not a decision.
4. **Parcelable-trio policy:** `writeToParcel` / `describeContents` / Parcel ctor / `$1.createFromParcel` / `$1.newArray` stay OPEN unless a printed app-side parcel/unparcel site is found. A CREATOR definition alone is not a caller, and a `$1.<init>` REACHABLE verdict does not imply `createFromParcel`/`newArray`.
5. **Library-dispatch equals:** `contains`/`containsAll`/`remove` dispatch to element `equals` by library code, so such rows are INFERRED, not SOURCE (row 24). An explicit printed `x.equals(y)` call is SOURCE (batch 29 row 34, batch 30 row 7).
6. **Room-generated instantiation policy:** a Room `@Entity`'s ctor or Parcel ctor is not flipped to REACHABLE on entity status alone; only a printed construction or a printed generated-instantiation step counts (rows 20, 22, 21 stay OPEN on this basis; row 8/10/32/38 are REACHABLE on printed `new` sites, not on entity status).
7. **Dependency rule:** a method whose only printed caller sits inside an OPEN method does not become REACHABLE. Illustrations: TagInfo Parcel ctor's only caller is the OPEN CREATOR (row 21/28); TorrentInfo Parcel ctor's `TagInfo.CREATOR` use (TorrentInfo.java:140) does not bootstrap TagInfo rows 19/27; Torrent 8-arg ctor's delegation to the 7-arg ctor (Torrent.java:98) is not a caller of the 8-arg ctor; AddTorrentParams parcel ctor (prior pass, OPEN) does not bootstrap TagInfo.writeToParcel (row 26).
8. **No OPEN → UNREACHABLE flip on absence** without a saved full-repo grep in rule-F/C5 form. The greps reported above are over the fetched subset only and prove nothing elsewhere.

## Gaps / named resolvers (all OPEN rows, plus the grep owed for any future UNREACHABLE claim)

- **Saved full-repo grep** (rule F/C5 form, covering generated code) for each OPEN row's exact call site, in particular: TagInfo ctors (rows 20, 22 — a `new TagInfo(` hit flips one of them to REACHABLE; Room-generated code would need its own printed step), Torrent 8-arg ctor (row 33 — a `new Torrent(` 8-arg hit), all Parcelable-trio rows (rows 1–3, 5, 6, 9, 11, 12–15, 17, 18, 21, 23, 25, 26, 28, 29, 31, 34), Torrent.equals/hashCode/toString (rows 35, 37, 40), FeedChannel.hashCode/toString (rows 1, 2), FeedItem.equals/hashCode/toString (rows 12, 13, 14).   the same file is REACHABLE (rows 7, 12, 26, 29); a public method whose only printed caller is
  itself (self-recursion) is OPEN, not REACHABLE (rows 8, 10) — recursion alone is not an entry route.

## Conventions used here — recorded, not decided

- `confirmed_by` follows the prior proposal's per-call-site convention: SOURCE = a printed
  app-code call site naming the method exists (including a call inside a listener/alert
  body whose dispatch is itself unprinted); INFERRED = the method is reached only through
  unprinted library/framework/Rx/JVM dispatch (alert callbacks, `types()`, lambdas,
  `<clinit>`). This is NOT the whole-route convention used in some earlier batches
  (e.g. batch 14), under which alert/Rx-route rows would all flip to INFERRED. The
  merger must pick one convention for the whole graph; labels below are unaffected by
  that choice, only `confirmed_by` would change. Rows that would flip under whole-route:
  rows 6, 11–16, 28, 35 (alert route) and row 24's Rx runnable (the
  `applySessionLoggerFilters` call itself is printed at TorrentSessionImpl.java:1388).
- Strict-entry caveat (unchanged from the prior proposal): routes anchor at
  TorrentService / TorrentEngine / TorrentSessionImpl / TorrentDownloadImpl, not at a
  printed launcher entry. The launcher-to-TorrentService step was NOT printed in this
  pass. Under a strict entry-route rule all REACHABLE rows below would revert to OPEN
  pending that printed step. This convention is also undecided at project level.
- Lambda/anonymous numbering is by source order and, where stated, by JVM-descriptor
  match — NOT bytecode-verified. A swap within a group would not change any label here.
- No UNREACHABLE label is claimed in this batch: no saved full-repo grep was run.

## Corrections to the audit (fresh print wins)

1. **NormalizeUrl.<init> (row 31) — audit reason disproved, verdict kept on weaker grounds.** The branch audit says "Private constructor explicitly disallows instantiation" citing NormalizeUrl.java:14-15. The fresh 423-line print has no explicit constructor at all: class at :33, static fields from :35, static normalize overloads at :165/:178. Rows 33–34 DO have the private-ctor pattern (PathResolver.java:14-16, PercentEncoder.java:20-22), which makes the NormalizeUrl claim look like a copy error. Row 31 is therefore proposed UNREACHABLE only on all-static use + no printed construction + the merger grep, NOT on a private ctor. If the merger requires a structural bar for ctor rows, row 31 reverts to OPEN.
2. **SafFileSystem line numbers — audit lines are off on the fresh copy; reasons hold.** Examples: delete(FakePath) is at :316-323 fresh (audit :314-323), exists(FakePath) at :344+ fresh (audit :329-366), openFD overloads at :382/:394/:404 fresh (audit :382-422 group), stat overloads at :426/:459/:474 fresh (audit :426-440, :459-470, :472-485). The table above cites fresh lines only.
3. **UriConverter (row 11) — audit OPEN upheld, not flipped.** Fresh print confirms the two converter methods are static (UriConverter.java:28-38) and AppDatabase registers the class (AppDatabase.java:53), but generated Room code is still absent, so the ctor route is still unproven. Per the task rule, OPEN is not flipped to UNREACHABLE without a saved full-repo grep / printed generated code. The plan's UNREACHABLE for this row is INFERRED only and is not adopted here.
4. **Interface citations (rows 1, 6, 7) are audit-file citations, not fresh fetches.** TorrentDownload.java:160, FeedRepository.java:38/:40 come from `batch_004_exact_calls.txt` as saved on the branch, not from a fresh connector fetch in this session. The merger should re-print those two interface files fresh if it wants SOURCE-grade citations for those rows; the labels do not depend on them (the implementation definitions are freshly printed).
5. **Utility ctor rows (31, 35–39) and SystemFacadeHelper (row 30) have no explicit ctor in the fresh prints.** Their UNREACHABLE proposals rest on all-static use + no printed construction + the merger grep, exactly as stated per row — not on a private ctor. Only rows 33–34 have a printed private ctor.

## Caveats for the merger

- **Strict-entry:** UNREACHABLE rows claim no route, so no entry step is asserted for them. If any row flips to REACHABLE on the merger grep, its entry route (launcher → activity/fragment → caller) must be printed fresh at that point; this file does not supply it.
- **Overload traps (do not merge by method name alone):** delete/exists/stat/openFD/getName/lastModified/normalize rows are all overload-sensitive — the table cites the exact signature and the exact dispatcher overload in every case. A grep must be signature-aware (e.g. `delete(FakePath` vs `delete(Uri`, `normalize(url, options` vs `normalize(url)`).
- **Chain rows (8–9–14, 13–16–29, 20–21–22):** these stand or fall together as printed. A single grep hit for getTorrentDataDir flips rows 8, 9 and 14 together; a hit for facade lastModified flips rows 13, 16 and 29 together; a hit for any SafFileSystem openFD overload flips rows 20–22 together (row 20 is the shared core overload).
- **Batch-54 interaction:** batch 54 left facade `buildValidFatFilename` + `isValidFatFilenameChar` + `trimFilename` OPEN. That does not change row 15 here (`isValidFatFilename` is a different method, and its caller — not its callee — is missing). If the merger closes batch 54's group as REACHABLE, re-check row 15 at that point, but do not flip it automatically.
- **Lambda/anonymous numbering:** not applicable — no lambda or anonymous-class rows in batch 4.
- **CONDITIONAL flip table — OPEN-closing round 2, row 11 (`UriConverter.<init>`) only.** Convention dependency: Room converter rule (xiii) family; this row is the converter-class ctor, NOT a `getRequiredConverters` row, so it is not part of any Room flip-together set. Mechanical application: (a) if the merger accepts the database-build assumption together with the printed facts (both converter methods static, class registered at AppDatabase.java:53, zero `new UriConverter` in the round-2 app-tree grep) as sufficient → row 11 → UNREACHABLE `no_trigger_in_app`, INFERRED; (b) if the merger requires printed generated `AppDatabase_Impl` converter code (the round-1 position, still the default) → row 11 stays OPEN until that print, and the §4.3-style converter dispatch for other batches does NOT automatically close it unless the merger says so explicitly; (c) a `new UriConverter` hit anywhere, or printed generated code instantiating the class → row 11 → REACHABLE (SOURCE if printed). Do not merge row 11 as UNREACHABLE on the app-tree grep alone while option (b) stands.
- **Not claimed:** no build, sampler, full-repo grep, or C1 was run or passed in this session. Build and C1 are marked "not run" (Mac, after push). No `search_code` 0-result was used as evidence of absence. No file outside `~/workspace/b4-src/` (printed source) and this file was written or edited; in particular `graph_def.py`, `out/`, `PROGRESS_V2`, `AGENT_NOTES`, and `tools/` were not touched, and nothing was pushed.
- **Spot-check note for the main agent:** suggested 1-in-5 sample (8 rows) covering each evidence kind: rows 11 (OPEN/generated), 17 (overload), 31 (corrected ctor), 33 (private ctor), 8 (private chain), 12 (missing facade forwarding), 3 (different-enum trap), 39 (utility ctor). The other 32 rows rest on the same printed files cited in the table.

  caller flips them to REACHABLE; finding none would support `closed_by_api_level`.
  They are NOT labelled UNREACHABLE here, because no saved full-repo grep was run.
  This is the method-vs-branch distinction from batches 73-74: a method whose *call site
  itself* never runs at API 27 is a closed_by_api_level candidate, whereas a method whose
  body merely returns early at API 27 (e.g. batch 74's applyLegacyNotifySettings) stays
  REACHABLE if its call site runs.

## Conventions recorded per row, NOT decided

- confirmed_by, per-row (this file): SOURCE = a printed app-code line naming the method
  (including a pref.* call site and an internal readSessionSettings call from the
  REACHABLE readSessionSettings route); INFERRED = the route crosses unprinted
  framework/library/Rx dispatch (observeSettingsChanged lambdas, rows 14-16) or rests
  on a printed gate with no ungated caller (UNREACHABLE rows, all INFERRED).
  Alternative convention, recorded not decided: every `pref` variable in the fetched
  files is typed to the SettingsRepository INTERFACE (TorrentEngine :104,
  TorrentService :82, TorrentNotifier :63, Utils :167/:639/:875/:894), and only the
  (not freshly fetched) RepositoryHelper local copy shows the singleton is a
  SettingsRepositoryImpl. Under an interface-dispatch convention, all SOURCE rows in
  this file would read INFERRED; labels are unaffected. Batch 43 merge-ready used
  SOURCE for the same pref.* pattern; batches 45/46 proposals used INFERRED. The
  merger must pick one convention and apply it across batches 43-46.
- Strict-entry: routes anchor at TorrentEngine/TorrentService/ViewModels/fragments, not
  at a printed launcher step, and fragment-anchored rows assume the fragment is
  displayed from Settings (that step was NOT printed). Under a strict printed-launcher
  rule all REACHABLE rows revert to OPEN. This does not change the UNREACHABLE rows
  (they have no route at any entry) or the OPEN rows.
- Lambda $ numbering (rows 14-16): matched by plan JVM descriptor, not by source order
  alone and not bytecode-verified: $0 (FlowableEmitter, SharedPreferences, String) is
  the OnSharedPreferenceChangeListener lambda (captured emitter, takes prefs + key);
  $1 (OnSharedPreferenceChangeListener) is the Disposables.fromAction unregister lambda
  (captures the listener); $2 (FlowableEmitter) is the Flowable.create emitter lambda.
  All three stand or fall with observeSettingsChanged (batch 45 row 17).
- Method-vs-call-site rule for the API-gated rows (17-20): applied exactly as in batch 45
  (notifySound/playSoundNotify). A method is closed_by_api_level here only because its
  ONLY printed call statements never execute at API 27 — either they sit inside a
  method that returns early at SDK >= O (TorrentNotifier :276, before :289-290), or
  inside a block that only runs at SDK < O (Appearance initLegacy, gate :118-119), or
  in an onPreferenceChange branch whose listener is bound only inside that gated block.
  This is not the "body returns early" case: these methods' own bodies are ordinary
  getters/setters; it is their call statements that are unreachable at API 27.
- Preference-persistence note (OPEN setters, rows 22/24/26/28/30/32): AndroidX preferences
  persist their own toggled values directly to SharedPreferences via the framework, so a
  changed preference value does NOT imply the app's programmatic setter ran. Only a
  printed programmatic setter call site counts as a route; for these six, such sites
  were printed 2026-11-01 in LogViewModel.java paramsCallback (see rows 22/24/26/28/30/32).
  LogSettingsFragment itself still prints only maxLogSize (:69/:95) — the log setters
  are NOT reached through LogSettingsFragment, they are reached through LogViewModel.

## Conventions recorded per row, NOT decided

- confirmed_by, per-row (this file): SOURCE = a printed app-code line naming the method;
  INFERRED = the route crosses unprinted framework/library/Rx dispatch (row 17, Rx
  subscription) or rests on a printed gate with no ungated caller (UNREACHABLE rows).
  This DIFFERS from the prior batch-45 proposal, which marked every row INFERRED on the
  ground that all `pref` variables are typed to the SettingsRepository interface
  (TorrentEngine :104, TorrentService :82, TorrentNotifier :63, Utils :167/:639) and
  only the (not freshly fetched) RepositoryHelper local copy shows the singleton is a
  SettingsRepositoryImpl. Under that interface-dispatch convention, all SOURCE rows here
  would read INFERRED; labels are unaffected. Batch 43 merge-ready used SOURCE for the
  same pref.* pattern. The merger must pick one convention and apply it across
  batches 43-46. This file uses SOURCE, consistent with batch 43 and batch 44.
- Strict-entry: routes anchor at TorrentEngine/TorrentService/fragments, not at a printed
  launcher step, and fragment-anchored rows assume the fragment is displayed from
  Settings (that step was NOT printed). Under a strict printed-launcher rule all
  REACHABLE rows revert to OPEN. This does not change the UNREACHABLE rows.
- Method-vs-call-site rule for the API-gated rows (15, 16, 20, 21): identical to batch 44
  rows 17-20. A method is closed_by_api_level here only because its ONLY printed call
  statements never execute at API 27 — inside a method that returns early at SDK >= O
  (TorrentNotifier :276, before :281-282), inside a block that only runs at SDK < O
  (Appearance initLegacy, gate :118-119), in a click listener registered only inside
  that gated block (notifySound picker launch, :144-168), or in an onPreferenceChange
  branch whose listener is bound only inside that gated block (:134). The methods' own
  bodies are ordinary getters/setters; it is their call statements that are unreachable
  at API 27. A saved full-repo grep is still required before merge (see below).

## Conventions recorded per row, NOT decided

- confirmed_by, per-row (this file): SOURCE = a printed app-code line naming the method.
  This DIFFERS from the prior batch-46 proposal, which marked every row INFERRED on the
  ground that all `pref` variables are typed to the SettingsRepository interface
  (TorrentEngine :104, Utils :167/:639/:703) and only the (not freshly fetched)
  RepositoryHelper local copy shows the singleton is a SettingsRepositoryImpl. Under
  that interface-dispatch convention, all SOURCE rows here would read INFERRED; labels
  are unaffected. Batch 43 merge-ready used SOURCE for the same pref.* pattern. The
  merger must pick one convention and apply it across batches 43-46. This file uses
  SOURCE, consistent with batch 43 and batches 44-45.
- Strict-entry: routes anchor at TorrentEngine/Utils/fragments, not at a printed launcher
  step, and fragment-anchored rows assume the fragment is displayed from Settings (that
  step was NOT printed). Under a strict printed-launcher rule all REACHABLE rows revert
  to OPEN.
- API-gate reading (rows 21-22, and why torrentFinishNotify rows 33/34 are NOT gated):
  torrentFinishNotify is NOT gated — its Appearance fragment calls at :91/:238 sit
  outside the SDK < O initLegacyNotifySettings block (gate :118-119), and the notifier
  call at :213 is ungated. The showManageAllFilesWarningDialog rows (21/22) ARE gated,
  by short-circuit, not just by the early return: Utils.showManageAllFilesWarningDialog
  :704-707 is `if (SDK_INT < R || hasPermission || !pref.showManageAllFilesWarningDialog())
  return;`. At standard API 27, the first disjunct SDK < R (27 < 30) is TRUE, so Java
  `||` short-circuits: the :706 getter is not even evaluated, and the :711 setter
  (after the return) is never reached. Both rows' only printed call sites are therefore
  dead at API 27, exactly as in batch 44 rows 17-20 / batch 45 rows 15/16/20/21 — hence
  closed_by_api_level proposed, pending a saved full-repo grep (an ungated caller flips
  either row to REACHABLE). This is consistent with the batch 73-74 lead that the
  ManageAllFilesWarningDialog rows on this same Utils method are closed_by_api_level
  candidates. Note the method itself (Utils.showManageAllFilesWarningDialog) still RUNS
  at API 27 via its MainActivity:180 caller (batch 73-74 print, not re-fetched here);
  it is the two pref.* call statements inside it that never execute at API 27.
